Logical characterisations, rule formats and compositionality for input-output conformance simulation Note: Research partially supported by the projects Nominal SOS (project nr. 141558-051) and Open Problems in the Equational Logic of Processes (project nr. 196050-051) of the Icelandic Research Fund; the Spanish projects DArDOS (TIN2015-65845-C3-1-R), TRACES (TIN2015-67522-C3-3-R) and Comunidad de Madrid FORTE-CM (P2018/TCS-4314); the project MATHADOR (COGS 724.464) of the European Research Council, and the Spanish addition to MATHADOR (TIN2016-81699-ERC). Journal: JLAMP
Luca Aceto, Ignacio Fábregas, Carlos Gregorio-Rodríguez, Anna Ingólfsdóttir

Abstract
Input-output conformance simulation ( 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 ​ 𝗌 ¯ \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}} ) has been proposed by Gregorio-Rodríguez, Llana and Martínez-Torres as a simulation-based behavioural preorder underlying model-based testing. This relation is inspired by Tretmans’ classic 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}} relation, but has better worst-case complexity than 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}} and supports stepwise refinement. The goal of this paper is to develop the theory of 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 ​ 𝗌 ¯ \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}} by studying logical characterisations of this relation, rule formats for it and its compositionality. More specifically, this article presents characterisations of 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 ​ 𝗌 ¯ \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}} in terms of modal logics and compares them with an existing logical characterisation for 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}} proposed by Beohar and Mousavi. It also offers a characteristic-formula construction for 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 ​ 𝗌 ¯ \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}} over finite processes in an extension of the proposed modal logics with greatest fixed points. A precongruence rule format for 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 ​ 𝗌 ¯ \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}} and a rule format ensuring that operations take quiescence properly into account are also given. Both rule formats are based on the GSOS format by Bloom, Istrail and Meyer. The general modal decomposition methodology of Fokkink and van Glabbeek is used to show how to check the satisfaction of properties expressed in the logic for 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 ​ 𝗌 ¯ \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}} in a compositional way for operations specified by rules in the precongruence rule format for 𝗂𝗈𝖼𝗈 ​ 𝗌 ¯ \mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}} .



1
Introduction

Model-based testing (MBT) is an increasingly popular technique for
validation and verification of computing systems, and provides a
compromise between formal verification approaches, such as model
checking, and manual testing. MBT uses a model to describe the aspects
of system behaviour that are considered to be relevant at some
suitable level of abstraction. This model is employed to generate test
cases automatically, while guaranteeing that some coverage criterion
is met. Such test cases are then executed on the actual system in
order to check whether its behaviour complies with that described by
the model.

A formal notion of compliance relation between models (specifications)
and systems (implementations) provides a formal underpinning for
MBT. The de-facto standard compliance relation underlying MBT for
labelled transition systems with input and output actions is the
classic
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
relation proposed by Tretmans, for which a whole MBT
framework and tools have been developed. (See, for
instance,
[
42
]
and the references therein. Readers interested
in an older and very influential strand of MBT research based
on finite-state machines can find a wealth of information in the
excellent survey paper
[
32
]
.)

An alternative conformance relation that can be used to underlie MBT is
input-output conformance simulation

(
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
). This relation follows many of the ideas in the definition of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
. However,
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
is a branching-time semantics
based on simulation, whereas
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
is a trace-based semantics.
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
has been introduced, motivated and proved to be
an adequate conformance relation for MBT in
[
20
,
21
,
22
]
.

Since
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
has been proposed as an alternative, branching-time
touchstone relation for MBT, it is natural to investigate its theory
in order to understand its properties. The goal of this paper is to
contribute to this endeavour by studying the discriminating power of

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
and its compositionality. More precisely, in
Section
3
, we provide modal characterisations of

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
in the style of Hennessy and Milner
[
25
]
. We offer two
modal chacterisations of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
, which are based on the use of
either a ‘non-forcing diamond modality’
(Theorem
2
) or of a ‘forcing box modality’
(Theorem
4
), and compare them with existing
logical characterisations for various semantics
(Section
3.1
) and a logic for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
proposed by Beohar
and Mousavi in
[
7
]
(Section
6.1
).
We also provide a characteristic formula construction for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}

(Proposition
15
in
Section
5
) for which we need an extension of
the logic for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
with fixed-points
(Section
4
), and show, by means of an example,
that, contrary to what is claimed in
[
33
, Theorem 2]
,
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}

and
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
do
not
coincide even when implementations are input
enabled (Section
6
).

As argued in
[
6
,
43
]
amongst other references, MBT
can benefit from a compositional approach whose goal is to increase
the efficiency of the testing activity. The above-mentioned references
study compositionality of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
with respect to a small collection
of well-chosen operations. Here we take a general approach to the
study of compositionality of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
, which is based on the theory of
rule formats for structural operational semantics
[
3
]
. In
Section
7.1
, we present a congruence rule format for

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
based on the GSOS format proposed by Bloom, Istrail and
Meyer
[
11
]
(Theorem
21
). Some of the
conditions of the rule format for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}

(Definition
16
) have similarities with those of the
rule format for
XY
-simulation given by Beohar and Mousavi
in
[
8
]
. However, our rule format for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
includes
a rather involved ‘global’ condition that stems from the fact that a
specification need only simulate input transitions from an
implementation that are labelled with actions that the specification
affords. In Section
7.1.1
, we present some
examples showing that the restrictions of the rule format from
Definition
16
cannot be relaxed easily.

A bridge between modal characterisations of process semantics and
rule formats for operational semantics is provided by the
so-called
modal decomposition
method of
[
17
]
,
whose roots can be traced back to the work by Larsen and
Liu
[
31
]
. Intuitively, this method allows one to
determine whether some process of the form
f(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})

satisfies a formula
\varphi
by constructing, from
\varphi
and
the rules defining the operation
f
, a collection of properties

\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}
such that
f(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})\models\varphi
if, and only if,
p_{i}\models\varphi_{i}
for

i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. This essentially amounts to providing a
compositional model-checking procedure and proof system for the
studied process logic with respect to operations specified by rules
in a given format.

In Section
7.2
we follow this approach for one of
the characterising logics for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
in order to obtain a
compositional proof system to decide whether a term built using
operations specified by rules in the
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
rule format satisfies a
formula (Theorem
22
). The conditions of the

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
rule format play a crucial role in the proof of correctness
of the given decomposition method.

Since operations preserving
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
need to take quiescence
into account properly, we also propose a rule format guaranteeing that
operations preserve coherent quiescent behaviour
(Theorem
23
in Section
7.3
),
and show that it is not easy to combine the rule formats for congruence
and quiescence (Proposition
24
). Finally,
Section
8
concludes the paper and presents avenues
for future research.

Some of the results in Sections
3
,

6
and
7
were presented in
the conference paper
[
2
]
. In this extended work we offer
proofs (some in the appendices) of the results that were announced
without proof in
[
2
]
and complement the study in those
sections with new results, examples and counterexamples. More in
detail, the present work contains the following new material.

•

In Section
3.1
, we provide a comparison
between the logics characterising
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
and some
well-known process logics.

•

In Section
4
, we develop an extension with fixed-points of the logics for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

•

In Section
5
, we define the characteristic formulae for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-processes.

•

In Section
7.1.1
, we show by means of examples that the conditions for the congruence rule
format cannot be relaxed easily.

•

In Section
7.2
, we apply the modal-decomposition method for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

•

In Proposition
24
, we show that is not
easy to combine the congruence and quiescence rule formats
by considering the example of the binary merge operator.

2
Preliminaries

The input-output conformance simulation preorder presented
in
[
20
,
21
,
23
]
(henceforth referred to as
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
)
is a semantic relation developed under the assumption that systems
have two kinds of actions: input actions, namely those that the
systems are willing to admit or respond to, and output actions, which
are those produced by the system and that can be seen as responses or
results.

We use
I
to denote the alphabet of input actions, which are written
with a question mark (
a?,b?,c?\ldots
). We call
O
the alphabet of
output actions, which are annotated with an exclamation mark
(
a!,b!,\delta!
…). In many cases we want to name actions in a
general sense, inputs and outputs indistinctly. We will consider the
set
L=I\cup O
and we will omit the exclamation or question marks
when naming generic actions,
a,b,c\in L
.

A state with no output actions cannot proceed autonomously; such a
state is called
quiescent
.
Following Tretmans
(see, for instance,
[
39
,
42
]
),
we directly introduce the event of quiescence
as a special output action denoted by
\delta!\in O
in the definition of
our models.

Definition 1
.

A
labelled transition system with inputs and outputs
, LTS for short, is a quadruple
(S,I,O,\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}})
such that

•

S
is a set of states, processes, or behaviours.

•

I
and
O
are disjoint sets of input and output actions,
respectively. Output actions include the quiescence symbol
\delta!\in O
. We define
L=I\cup O
.

•

\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}\subseteq S\times L\times S
is the transition relation.
As usual, we write
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q
instead of
(p,a,q)\in\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
and

p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
, for
a\in L
, if there exists some
q\in S
such that

p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q
. Analogously, we will write

p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
, for
a\in L
, if there is no
q
such that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q
.

In order to allow only for coherent quiescent systems, the set of
transitions
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
should also satisfy the following requirement:

p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
iff
p=p^{\prime}
and
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
for each
a!\in O\setminus\{\delta!\}
.

The extension of the transition relation to sequences of actions is
defined as usual.

Contrary to the classic
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
testing theory, in the theory of

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
presented in
[
20
,
21
,
22
,
23
]
, all actions are
assumed to be observable. In this paper, we follow those references
and consider only concrete actions.

In general we use
p,q,p^{\prime},q^{\prime}
… for states or behaviours, but also
i,i^{\prime},s
and
s^{\prime}
when we want to emphasise the
specific role of a behaviour as an implementation or a specification, respectively. We consider implementations and
specifications, or, more generally, behaviours under study, as states of the same LTS.

The following functions over states of an LTS will be used in the remainder of the paper:

\mathsf{outs}(p)

=

\{a!\ |\ a!\in O,\ p\ \mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}\}
, the set of initial outputs of a state
p
.

\mathsf{ins}(p)

=

\{a?\ |\ a?\in I,\ p\ \mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}\}
, the set of initial
inputs of a state
p
.

The definition of input-output conformance simulation given below
stems from
[
20
,
21
,
23
]
, to which we refer the interested
reader for motivation and discussion.

Definition 2
.

We say that a binary relation
R
over states in an LTS is an
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-relation if, and only if, for each
(p,q)\in R

the following conditions hold:

1.

\mathsf{ins}(q)\subseteq\mathsf{ins}(p)
.

2.

For all
a?\in\mathsf{ins}(q)
and
p^{\prime}\in S
, if
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
then there exists some
q^{\prime}
such that
q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}
and
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in R
.

3.

For all
a!\in O
and
p^{\prime}\in S
, if
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
then there exists some
q^{\prime}
such that

q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}
and
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in R
.

We define the
input-output conformance simulation
(
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
)
as the largest
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-relation, that is, the union of all
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-relations:

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}=\bigcup\{R\ |\ R\subseteq S\times S,\ R\ \text{is an $\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}$-relation}\}.

We write
p\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}q
instead of
(p,q)\in\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

Example 1
.

Consider the following processes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
a?
\delta!
\delta!

It is easy to see that
i~{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}~s
. Indeed,
\mathsf{ins}(s)=\emptyset
and
therefore the specification
s
does not prevent the implementation
i

from offering the input transition
i\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}i
.

Remark 1
.

In what follows, we will consider only
image-finite

LTSs, that is, LTSs where for each
p
and each
a\in I\cup O

there are only finitely many
p^{\prime}
such that

p\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\rightarrow}}p^{\prime}
. Also, we will consider both

I
and
O
to be finite sets.

Throughout the paper we make extensive use of modal logics. A logic
over processes is defined by a language to express the formulae in the
logic and a satisfaction relation that defines when a process (that
is, a state of an LTS) has the property described by some formula. A
classic example and a reference for the rest of the paper is
Hennessy-Milner Logic
[
25
]
.

Definition 3
.

Hennessy-Milner Logic over the set of actions
L
(abbreviated to

HML
) is the collection of formulae defined by the following BNF
grammar:

\phi::=\mathbf{tt}\mid\mathbf{ff}\mid\phi\wedge\phi\mid\phi\vee\phi\mid[\,a\,]\phi\mid\langle a\rangle\phi,

where
a\in L
.
HML
is interpreted over an LTS by defining a
satisfaction relation
\models
relating states to formulae. The
semantics of the boolean constants
\mathbf{tt}
and
\mathbf{ff}
and of the
boolean connectives
\wedge
and
\vee
is defined as usual. The
satisfaction relation for the modalities
\langle a\rangle
and
[\,a\,]
is
as follows:

•

p\models\langle a\rangle\varphi
iff there exists some
p^{\prime}
such that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
and
p^{\prime}\models\varphi
.

•

p\models[\,a\,]\varphi
iff for all
p^{\prime}
,

p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
implies
p^{\prime}\models\varphi
.

As usual, we extend both boolean connectives
\wedge
and
\vee
to finite sets: given a finite index set
I
and
formulae
\varphi_{i}
(
i\in I
), we define
\bigwedge_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}
(respectively,
\bigvee_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}
) as the
finite conjunction (respectively, finite disjunction) of the formulae
\varphi_{i}
. We follow the standard convention of
considering
\bigwedge_{i\in\emptyset}\varphi_{i}
equivalent to
\mathbf{tt}
and
\bigvee_{i\in\emptyset}\varphi_{i}

equivalent to
\mathbf{ff}
.

Every subset of
HML
naturally induces a preorder on a given set of
behaviours.

Definition 4
.

Given a logic
\mathcal{L}
included in
HML
and a set
S
of
states in an LTS, we define
\leq_{\mathcal{L}}
as the
binary relation over
S
given by

p\leq_{\mathcal{L}}q\quad\mbox{ iff }\quad\forall\phi\in\mathcal{L}\;\;(p\models\phi~{\Rightarrow}~q\models\phi).

Proposition 1
.

For each logic
\mathcal{L}
, the binary relation
\leq_{\mathcal{L}}
is a preorder.

3
Logic for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}

In this section we present logics that characterise the
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
relations, both the preorder and its induced equivalence.
These logics are subsets of Hennessy-Milner Logic (HML) and are convenient to characterise clearly the
discriminating power of the
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
relation. We will use these logics to compare
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
with other behavioural
simulation-based relations in the literature in Sections
3.1
and
6
. In
Section
4
, we will address the study of more expressive logics for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
that are more suitable for
the description of system properties.

Definition 5
.

The syntax of the logic for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
, denoted by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
, is defined by the following BNF grammar:

\phi::=\mathbf{tt}\mid\mathbf{ff}\mid\phi\wedge\phi\mid\phi\vee\phi\mid\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\phi\mid\langle a!\rangle\phi,

where
a?\in I
and
a!\in O
. The semantics of the constants
\mathbf{tt}

and
\mathbf{ff}
, of the boolean connectives
\wedge
(conjunction) and
\vee
(disjunction), and of
the modality
\langle a!\rangle
(diamond) are defined as usual. The satisfaction
relation for the modality
\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle
is given below:

p\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\phi\mbox{ iff }p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}\mbox{ or }p^{\prime}\models\phi\mbox{ for some }p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}.

The new modal operator
\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle
can be read as a

non-forcing
diamond modality: if the input action labelling the
modality is not possible in a given state then the formula is
satisfied. This operator can be expressed with the classic modalities
in
HML
; indeed,
\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\phi
is equivalent to
\langle a?\rangle\phi\vee[\,a?\,]\mathbf{ff}
. The need for this special modality arises because,
in order for
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
to hold,
s
need only match the input
transitions of
i
that are labelled with input actions that
s

affords.

Remark 2
.

Note that the formula
\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\mathbf{ff}
is logically
equivalent to the HML formula
[\,a?\,]\mathbf{ff}
. In other
words,
p\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\mathbf{ff}
iff
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
.

According to Definition
4
, the logic
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
induces the preorder
\leq_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}
. Next we prove that
this logical preorder coincides with the input-output conformance simulation preorder,
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
, over an arbitrary
(image-finite) LTS.

Theorem 2
.

For all states
i,s
in some LTS,

i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s\quad\mbox{iff}\quad i\leq_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}s.

Proof.

We prove the two implications separately.

•

Only if implication
: Assume that
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
and
i\models\phi
with
\phi\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
. We show that
s\models\phi
by
structural induction over the formula
\phi
. We limit ourselves to
presenting the case that
\phi=\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\varphi
, for some

\varphi
.

Since
s\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\varphi
holds when

s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
, in what follows we may assume that
a?\in\mathsf{ins}(s)
. Since
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
, by
Definition
2
.
1
,
\mathsf{ins}(s)\subseteq\mathsf{ins}(i)
. So, from
i\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\varphi
we obtain that there
exists some
i^{\prime}
such that
i\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}i^{\prime}
and
i^{\prime}\models\varphi
.
By Definition
2
.
2
, we have that there
exists some
s^{\prime}
such that
s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}s^{\prime}
such that
i^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s^{\prime}
. Now, since
i^{\prime}\models\varphi
, by the induction hypothesis, we
have also that
s^{\prime}\models\varphi
. That is,

s\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\varphi
, which was to be shown.

•

If implication
: Consider the relation

R=\{(i,s)\mid i\leq_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}s\}
. We claim that
R
is an

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-relation. We will prove that claim by contradiction.

Assume
that
(i,s)\in R
does not satisfy the requirements in
Definition
2
. We will see that there exists a formula

\varphi
in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
such that
i\models\varphi
and

s\not\models\varphi
, contradicting the assumption that
i\leq_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}s
. We distinguish three cases according to the
conditions in Definition
2
.

–

Assume that

\mathsf{ins}(s)\not\subseteq\mathsf{ins}(i)
. Then, there
exists some

a?\in\mathsf{ins}(s)\setminus\mathsf{ins}(i)
. In this
case, using Remark
2
,

i\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\mathbf{ff}
but

s\not\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\mathbf{ff}
.

–

Assume that there exist
a?\in\mathsf{ins}(s)\cap\mathsf{ins}(i)
and
i^{\prime}
such that
i\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}i^{\prime}
and
(i^{\prime},s^{\prime})\notin R
for each
s^{\prime}
such that
s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}s^{\prime}
. Since for each
s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}s^{\prime}
we have
(i^{\prime},s^{\prime})\notin R
, there exist formulae
\varphi_{s^{\prime}}
such that
i^{\prime}\models\varphi_{s^{\prime}}
and
s^{\prime}\not\models\varphi_{s^{\prime}}
. Let
\varphi=\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\bigwedge_{s\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a?}}{{\rightarrow}}s^{\prime}}\varphi_{s^{\prime}}
. By construction
i\models\varphi
and
s\not\models\varphi
.

–

Assume that there exist
a!\in O
and
i^{\prime}
such that
i\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}i^{\prime}
and
(i^{\prime},s^{\prime})\notin R
for all
s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}s^{\prime}
. As above, for each
s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}s^{\prime}
there exists some formula
\varphi_{s^{\prime}}
such that
i^{\prime}\models\varphi_{s^{\prime}}
and
s^{\prime}\not\models\varphi_{s^{\prime}}
. In this case, the formula

\varphi=\langle a!\rangle\bigwedge_{s\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a!}}{{\rightarrow}}s^{\prime}}\varphi_{s^{\prime}}
is such that
i\models\varphi
but

s\not\models\varphi
. ∎

Remark 3
.

An easy consequence of the above logical-characterisation theorem is
that there is no formula in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
that is logically equivalent to
the HML formula
\langle a?\rangle\mathbf{tt}
. Indeed, that formula is satisfied by
state
i
, but not by state
s
, in Example
1
.

The
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
relation is a preorder and it induces the equivalence relation,

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}_{\equiv}}}
. We can logically characterise this equivalence with the following result.

Corollary 3
.

For all states
p,q
in some LTS,

p\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}_{\equiv}}}q\quad\mbox{iff}\quad(\forall\phi\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}\quad p\models\phi\mbox{ iff }q\models\phi).

The logic for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
we have presented in Definition
5

follows a standard approach to the logical characterisation of
simulation semantics; see, for instance,
[
15
,
18
]
. However,
the
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
relation originated in the model-based testing environment
where the natural reading for a logical characterisation would be

‘every property satisfied by the specification should also hold
in the implementation.’
Next we define an
alternative logic that better matches this
specification/implementation view.

Definition 6
.

The syntax of the logic
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
is defined by the following BNF grammar:

\phi::=\mathbf{tt}\mid\mathbf{ff}\mid\phi\wedge\phi\mid\phi\vee\phi\mid\llbracket a?\rrbracket\phi\mid[\,a!\,]\phi,

where
a?\in I
and
a!\in O
. The semantics of the constants
\mathbf{tt}
and
\mathbf{ff}
, of the boolean connectives

\wedge
and
\vee
, and of the modality
[\,a!\,]
is defined as usual. The satisfaction relation for the
modalities
\llbracket a?\rrbracket
is as follows:

p\models\llbracket a?\rrbracket\phi\mbox{ iff }p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}\mbox{ and }p^{\prime}\models\phi,\mbox{ for each }p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}.

The new modal operator, denoted by
\llbracket a?\rrbracket
, can be read as a

forcing
box modality: the action specified in the modality must
be possible in order for a process to satisfy the formula. This
operator can be described with the classic modalities in
HML
:

\llbracket a?\rrbracket\phi
is equivalent to
\langle a?\rangle\mathbf{tt}\wedge[\,a?\,]\phi
.

Remark 4
.

Notice that the formula
\llbracket a?\rrbracket\mathbf{tt}
is logically equivalent to
\langle a?\rangle\mathbf{tt}
. In other words,
p\models\llbracket a?\rrbracket\mathbf{tt}
iff
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
.

Now with this logic, we can define a preorder
\leq_{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}
in terms of
the formulae that the specification satisfies:
s\leq_{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}i\quad\mbox{iff}\quad\forall\phi\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}\quad({s\models\phi}\Rightarrow{i\models\phi})
.

We note that the logics
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
are dual. In fact, there exist mutual transformations between both
sets of formulae such that a behaviour satisfies one formula if, and only if, it does
not
satisfy the transformed
formula. These statements are at the heart of the proof of the following result, as we will show below.

Theorem 4
.

For all states
i,s
in some LTS,
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s~\mbox{iff}~s\leq_{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}i.

Definition 7
.

We define the bijection
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
by induction
on the structure of formulae in the following way:

•

\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{tt})=\mathbf{ff}
.

•

\mathcal{T}(\mathbf{ff})=\mathbf{tt}
.

•

\mathcal{T}(\phi_{1}\wedge\phi_{2})=\mathcal{T}(\phi_{1})\vee\mathcal{T}(\phi_{2})
.

•

\mathcal{T}(\phi_{1}\vee\phi_{2})=\mathcal{T}(\phi_{1})\wedge\mathcal{T}(\phi_{2})
.

•

\mathcal{T}(\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\phi)=\llbracket a?\rrbracket\mathcal{T}(\phi)
.

•

\mathcal{T}(\langle a!\rangle\phi)=[\,a!\,]\mathcal{T}(\phi)
.

The inverse function
\mathcal{T}^{-1}:\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
is defined
in the obvious way.

In order to prove Theorem
4
, we first prove the
following lemma to the effect that a behaviour satisfies some formula

\phi
if, and only if, it does not satisfy the transformed formula

\mathcal{T}(\phi)
.

Lemma 5
.

For each state
p
in some LTS, and formula

\phi\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
, we have
that

(i)

if

p\models\phi
then
p\not\models\mathcal{T}(\phi)
, and

(ii)

if
p\not\models\phi
then
p\models\mathcal{T}(\phi)
.

Proof.

We prove both statements by structural induction over the formula
\phi\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
. We limit ourselves to giving the proof for the case
\phi=\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\varphi
. By definition
\mathcal{T}(\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\varphi)=\llbracket a?\rrbracket\mathcal{T}(\varphi)
.

(
i
): Consider some process
p
such that
p\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\varphi
. Since
p\models\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\varphi
we have that either
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
, or there exists
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
such that
p^{\prime}\models\varphi
. If
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
, then
p\not\models\llbracket a?\rrbracket\mathcal{T}(\varphi)
. Assume now that there exists
some
p^{\prime}
such that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
and
p^{\prime}\models\varphi
. By the inductive hypothesis,

p^{\prime}\not\models\mathcal{T}(\varphi)
. Therefore
p\not\models\llbracket a?\rrbracket\mathcal{T}(\varphi)
, and we are done.

(
ii
): Consider, on the other hand, some process
p
such that
p\not\models\phi
. This means that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
and
p^{\prime}\not\models\varphi
for all
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
. By the induction hypothesis,
p^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}(\varphi)
for each
p^{\prime}
such that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
. Since
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
, we obtain that
p\models\llbracket a?\rrbracket\mathcal{T}(\varphi)
, thus concluding the proof.
∎

The following corollary will be useful in the proof of
Theorem
4
.

Corollary 6
.

For every state
p
in some LTS, and all formulae
\phi\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
and
\psi\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
, the following properties hold:

(i)

p\models\phi
iff
p\not\models\mathcal{T}(\phi)
.

(ii)

p\models\psi
iff
p\not\models\mathcal{T}^{-1}(\psi)
.

Finally, we have all the ingredients we need in order to prove
Theorem
4
, which is the equivalent result to
Theorem
2
for
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Assume that
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
and let us consider
\phi\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
such that
s\models\phi
. By
Corollary
6
.(ii),
s\not\models\mathcal{T}^{-1}(\phi)
, with
\mathcal{T}^{-1}(\phi)\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
. Now, by
Theorem
2
, this implies
i\not\models\mathcal{T}^{-1}(\phi)
and by
Lemma
5
.
ii
,
i\models\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{T}^{-1}(\phi))=\phi
.

On the other hand, let us suppose that for all
\phi\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
, if
s\models\phi
then
i\models\phi
. By
Theorem
2
to show that
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
it suffices to prove that
i\models\varphi
implies

s\models\varphi
for each
\varphi\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
. To this end, let us suppose that
i\models\varphi
. By
Corollary
6
.(i), we have that
i\not\models\mathcal{T}(\varphi)
. Now, by hypothesis, since

\mathcal{T}(\varphi)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
it must also be the case that
s\not\models\mathcal{T}(\varphi)
. Applying again
Corollary
6
.(i) we obtain that
s\models\varphi
. Thus, we conclude that
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
,
finishing the proof.
∎

As we did in Corollary
3
for
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
, we can also easily characterise the equivalence relation
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}_{\equiv}}}
by using
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
.

Corollary 7
.

For all states
p,q
in some
\mathit{LTS}
,

p\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}_{\equiv}}}q\quad\mbox{iff}\quad(\forall\phi\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}\quad p\models\phi\mbox{ iff }q\models\phi).

From Corollaries
3
and
7

we can also tell that
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}_{\equiv}}}
can be logically characterised by means
of boolean combinations of the formulae in the logics
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
and

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
. This means that we could write formulae that are in

\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
or in
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
and make conjunctions and disjunctions
between them, but not nest in the same formula modal operators from

\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
. That is,
\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle\mathbf{tt}\wedge[\,x!\,]\mathbf{ff}

would be acceptable, but
\langle\!|a?|\!\rangle[\,x!\,]\mathbf{ff}
, would not. This is
reminiscent of the case of mutual simulation and its logical
characterisation as opposed to bisimulation equivalence and its
logical characterisation
[
15
,
18
]
.

Definition 8
.

The logic
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}^{\equiv}
is
defined by the following BNF grammar:

\phi::=\psi_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}\mid\psi_{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}\mid\phi\vee\phi\mid\phi\wedge\phi,

where
\psi_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
and
\psi_{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
.

Corollary 8
.

For all states
p,q
in some LTS,

p\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}_{\equiv}}}q\mbox{ iff }(\forall\phi\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}^{\equiv}\quad p\models\phi\mbox{ iff }q\models\phi).

3.1
Related logics

Logics provide a systematic
way to compare behavioural relations. In this section
we relate the characterisations for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
presented in Section
3
