q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}
such that
(\sigma_{R},p^{\prime})\models X_{q^{\prime}}
, that is, for every
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
, there exists some
q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}
such that
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in R
.

Finally, let us consider
a!\in O
, since
(\sigma_{R},p)\models[\,a!\,]\bigvee_{q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-2.3917pt\hbox to5.38892pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-2.3917pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-3.98611pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-2.3917pt\hbox to5.38892pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-2.3917pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-3.98611pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-2.3917pt\hbox to5.38892pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-2.3917pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-3.98611pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-2.3917pt\hbox to5.38892pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-2.3917pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-3.98611pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}}X_{q^{\prime}}
, we also have that
(\sigma_{R},p^{\prime})\models\bigvee_{q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-2.3917pt\hbox to5.38892pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-2.3917pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-3.98611pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-2.3917pt\hbox to5.38892pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-2.3917pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-3.98611pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-2.3917pt\hbox to5.38892pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-2.3917pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-3.98611pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-2.3917pt\hbox to5.38892pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-2.3917pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-3.98611pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}}X_{q^{\prime}}
for every
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
. Hence, for each
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
there exists
q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}
such that
(\sigma_{R},p^{\prime})\models X_{q^{\prime}}
, that is, such that
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in R
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the light of Lemma
16
, we can apply Corollary 3.4 in
[
4
]
to prove Proposition
15
, which gives the explicit definition of the
characteristic formula for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

6
The relation between
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
and
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}

Input-output conformance (
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
) was introduced by Tretmans
in
[
41
]
. The intuition behind
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
is that a process
i
is a
correct implementation of a specification
s
if, for each sequence of
actions
\sigma
allowed by the specification, all the possible
outputs from
i
after having performed
\sigma
are allowed by the
specification. This is formalised below in a setting in which all
actions are observable.

Definition 15
.

Let
(S,I,O,\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to3.33333pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}})
be an LTS with inputs and outputs. We define the
traces of a state
p\in S
as
\mathsf{traces}(p)=\{\sigma\mid\exists p^{\prime}.~p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}\}
. Given a trace
\sigma
, we define

p~\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}~\sigma=\{p^{\prime}\mid p^{\prime}\in S,~p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}\}
. For each
T\subseteq S
, we set
\mathsf{Out}(T)=\bigcup_{p\in T}\mathsf{outs}(p)
. Finally, the
relation
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}\subseteq S\times S
is defined as:

i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}s\textrm{ iff }\mathsf{Out}(i\,\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}\,\sigma)\subseteq\mathsf{Out}(s\,\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}\,\sigma),\textrm{ for all
}\sigma\in\mathsf{traces}(s).

As shown in
[
20
, Theorem 1]
,
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
is included in
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
.

In the setting of Tretmans’ standard
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
theory
[
41
]
, only input-enabled implementations are
considered. We recall that a state
i
in an LTS is input enabled if every state
i^{\prime}
that is reachable from
i

is able to perform every input action, that is,
i^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
holds for each
a?\in I
.

Theorem
2
in
[
33
]
states that if
i
is input enabled,
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}s
implies
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
. This means
that, when restricted to input-enabled implementations,
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
and
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
coincide, and therefore the
logics characterising
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
presented in this paper also characterise
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
over that class of LTSs.
Unfortunately, however, Theorem
2
in
[
33
]
does
not
hold, as shown in the following example.

Example 3
.

Let
s
and
i
be defined as follows, where we assume that
I=\{a?,b?\}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
a?
a?
\delta!
b!
a!
\delta!
\delta!
b?
a?
a?,b?
b!
a!
\delta!,a?,b?

Note that
i
is input-enabled, as required by the theory of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
. It
is easy to see that
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}s
. On the other hand,
i\mathbin{\kern 10.00002pt\hbox to0.0pt{/\hss}\kern-10.00002pt\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s

because each
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
relation containing the pair
(i,s)
would also
have to contain the pair
(i^{\prime},s_{1})
or the pair
(i^{\prime},s_{2})
. However,
no relation including either of those pairs is an
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-relation
because
i^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
and
i^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
, but
s_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a!\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a!\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
and

s_{2}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b!\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b!\kern 2.45836pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b!\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\not\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b!\kern 2.20837pt$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
.

However, as one might expect, Theorem
2
in
[
33
]
becomes true
when the specification
s
is deterministic. An LTS is deterministic
whenever there is only one possible successor for each action, that
is, if for all
p,p^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}\in S
and
a\in L
, if
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
and

p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime\prime}
, then
p^{\prime}=p^{\prime\prime}
.

Proposition 17
.

Let
i
be an input-enabled LTS and
s
be a deterministic LTS. If
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}s
then
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
.

Proof.

Assume that
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}s
. We will show that the relation

R=\{(p,q)\mid\exists\sigma\in\mathsf{traces}(s)\text{ such that }p\in i\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}\sigma\text{ and }q\in s\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}\sigma\}

is a
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-relation. First, we observe that
(i,s)\in R
since
\varepsilon\in\mathsf{traces}(s)
. Now, we show that any pair
(p,q)\in R
satisfies the conditions of Definition
2
.

•

Trivially,
\mathsf{ins}(q)\subseteq\mathsf{ins}(p)
. Indeed, since
i
is input-enabled,
\mathsf{ins}(q)\subseteq I=\mathsf{ins}(p)
.

•

Let
a?\in\mathsf{ins}(q)
and assume that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
for some
p^{\prime}
. Since
a?\in\mathsf{ins}(q)
, by
definition there exists some
q^{\prime}
such that
q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}
, so
\sigma a?\in\mathsf{traces}(s)
. Hence,
p^{\prime}\in i\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}\sigma a?
and
q^{\prime}\in s\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}\sigma a?
, that is,
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in R
.

•

Let
b!\in O
be such that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
, that is,
b!\in\mathsf{outs}(p)\subseteq\mathsf{Out}(i~{\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}}~\sigma)
.
Now, since
i~{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}}~s
and
\sigma\in\mathsf{traces}(s)
, we have that
\mathsf{Out}(i~{\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}}~\sigma)\subseteq\mathsf{Out}(s~{\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}}~\sigma)
. Hence,
b!\in\mathsf{Out}(s\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}\sigma)
and, since
s
is deterministic,

b!\in\mathsf{outs}(q)
so there exists some
q^{\prime}
such that
q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}q^{\prime}
. Summing up, we have that
\sigma b!\in\mathsf{traces}(s)
,

p^{\prime}\in i~{\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}}~\sigma b!
and
q^{\prime}\in s~{\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}}~\sigma b!
, that is,
(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})\in R
. ∎

Remark 8
.

The construction of the relation
R
used in the proof of the above
proposition is akin to the definition of ‘coinductive
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
’ employed in
the proof of Theorem 3 in
[
36
]
. In fact, the use of such
relations in the algorithmics of decorated trace semantics, such as

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
, as well as failure and testing equivalences, can be traced at
least as far back as
[
13
]
.

6.1
The relation with a logic for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}

In
[
7
]
Beohar and Mousavi introduced an explicit
logical characterisation of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
. This characterisation uses a
non-standard modal operator reminiscent of our
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
,
denoted by
\llfloor\cdot\rrfloor
1
1

1

In fact, the symbol used to denote
the operator
\llfloor\cdot\rrfloor
in
[
7
]
is
\langle\![\cdot]\!\rangle
, but we prefer to use an alternative notation in order
to avoid confusion with our modal operator
\langle\!|\cdot|\!\rangle
.
. However, output actions can also be used as labels of
\llfloor\cdot\rrfloor
.
This modality can be extended to traces
\sigma
as follows:
p\models\llfloor\sigma\rrfloor\phi
if, and only if,
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
and
p^{\prime}\models\phi
, for each
p^{\prime}
such that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
. (Note that, for the
particular case of input actions
a?
, the semantics of
\llfloor a?\rrfloor

coincides with that of
\llbracket a?\rrbracket
.)

The explicit logical characterisation of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
given
in
[
7
]
is defined by means of two
different subclasses of logical formulae. The first subclass permits
only formulae of the form
\llfloor\sigma\rrfloor[\,b\,]\mathbf{ff}
, where
\sigma

is a trace and
b
is an output action.

For the second subclass of formulae, Beohar and Mousavi consider the natural
extension of the operator
[\,\cdot\,]
to traces, defined as:
p\models[\,\sigma\,]\phi
if, and only if,
p^{\prime}\models\phi
for each

p^{\prime}
such that
p\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sigma$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}p^{\prime}
. This second subclass permits only
formulae of the form
[\,\sigma\,][\,b\,]\mathbf{ff}
, where
\sigma
is a
trace and
b
is an output action.

The formulae in each of these two subclasses characterise one defining
property of the
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
-relation. This intuition is made precise in the
following lemma.

Lemma 18

(
[
7
]
)
.

For each sequence of actions
\sigma
, output action
b
and
process
p
the following statements hold:

1.

\sigma\in\mathsf{traces}(p)
and
b\notin\mathsf{Out}(p~\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}~\sigma)
iff
p\models\llfloor\sigma\rrfloor[\,b\,]\mathbf{ff}
.

2.

b\notin\mathsf{Out}(p~\mathbin{\mathsf{after}}~\sigma)
iff
p\models[\,\sigma\,][\,b\,]\mathbf{ff}
.

The resulting logical characterisation theorem by Beohar and Mousavi for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
is as follows.

Theorem 19

(
[
7
]
)
.

i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}s
iff, for all
\sigma\in L^{*}
,
b\in O
, if
s\models\llfloor\sigma\rrfloor[\,b\,]\mathbf{ff}
, then
i\models[\,\sigma\,][\,b\,]\mathbf{ff}
.

Theorem
4
in this paper is the counterpart of the above result for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
. Note, however,
that Theorem
19
is not a classic modal characterisation result (as it is the case of, for
example, Theorem
4
) where if the implementation
i
is correct with respect to the
specification
s
and
s
satisfies a formula, then also
i
satisfies it. Here the implementation does not
need to satisfy the properties that hold for the specification. By way of example, implementations need not
exhibit all the traces of a specification they correctly implement.

As we will now argue, the logics for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
and
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
are incomparable in terms of their expressive power.
First of all, note that, if we consider only input-enabled implementations, the formulae of the form

[\,\sigma\,][\,b\,]\mathbf{ff}
, with
\sigma
a trace, can be expressed in
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
since in an
input-enabled scenario
\llbracket a?\rrbracket
has the same semantics as
[\,a?\,]
. On the other hand, it is not
possible to define a formula
\phi\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
that captures Lemma
18
(
1
). Indeed,
by way of example, consider
\phi=\llfloor x!\rrfloor[\,b!\,]\mathbf{ff}
. Any specification
s
would have to satisfy

\phi
iff
s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle x!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle x!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}
and
s^{\prime}\models[\,b!\,]\mathbf{ff}
, for all
s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle x!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle x!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.00003pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.00003pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x!$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}s^{\prime}
. Now, assume that we have in

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
a formula
\psi
whose semantics coincides with that of
\llfloor x!\rrfloor[\,b!\,]\mathbf{ff}
. Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
x!
a!
a!

It is easy to see that
i~\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}~s
, but
s\models\psi
and
i\not\models\psi
. In other words,
\psi
is a formula that distinguishes processes related by
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
. Hence, such a formula
\psi
cannot be expressed in any logic that characterises
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

On the other hand, let us consider the two processes of Example
3
and the formula
\phi=\llbracket a?\rrbracket([\,a!\,]\mathbf{ff}\vee[\,b!\,]\mathbf{ff})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
. As we already stated in Example
3
,
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}s
, but
s\models\phi
and
i\not\models\phi
. Hence,
\phi
can distinguish processes that are
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
-related.

Now, since
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
implies
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}

[
20
]
, we can use
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}^{\nu}
(see Section
4
) to express distinguishing formulae whenever two processes are not
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
-related.

Proposition 20
.

If
i\mathbin{\kern 10.00002pt\hbox to0.0pt{/\hss}\kern-10.00002pt\mathsf{ioco}}s
, then there exists a formula
\psi\in\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}^{\nu}
such that
s\models\psi
but
i\not\models\psi
.

Proof.

Straightforward from the fact that
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}s
implies
i\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}s
, the logical characterisations of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
in Theorem
2
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
in Theorem
4
.
∎

Remark 9
.

As we commented on Remark
6
, a dual logic to
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}^{\nu}
is

\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}^{\mu}
. Therefore, analogously, if
i\mathbin{\kern 10.00002pt\hbox to0.0pt{/\hss}\kern-10.00002pt\mathsf{ioco}}\ s
, then there exists a
formula
\phi\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}^{\mu}
such that
i\models\phi
but
s\not\models\phi
.

Proposition
20
can be applied, in particular, for
\chi_{s}
, the characteristic formula
of
s
(Definition
13
). Hence, if
i\mathbin{\kern 10.00002pt\hbox to0.0pt{/\hss}\kern-10.00002pt\mathsf{ioco}}\ s
, we obtain that
s\models\chi_{s}

and
i\not\models\chi_{s}
. That is, the characteristic formula of
s
can be used as a distinguishing formula.
This way we have an alternative, albeit incomplete, criterion for checking if an implementation does not
conform to a specification with respect to
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
:

(i)

first, build
\chi_{s}
, the characteristic formula for
s
;

(ii)

check if
i\models\chi_{s}
(
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
satisfaction);

(iii)

now, if
i\not\models\chi_{s}
then, we have that
i\mathbin{\kern 10.00002pt\hbox to0.0pt{/\hss}\kern-10.00002pt\mathsf{ioco}}s
.

7
Rule formats for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}

In this section we focus on the study of structural properties of

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
, with emphasis on its compositionality and on
compositional proof systems for its characterising logic
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
.
We start, in Section
7.1
, by defining a
precongruence rule format for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
. That rule format is shown to
ensure compositionality with respect to
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
for a subset of the
operators defined using rules in the GSOS format proposed by Bloom,
Istrail and Meyer
[
11
]
. The rule format provides a sufficient
condition for compositionality; we show with counterexamples how the
rules we propose cannot be easily relaxed without jeopardizing the
compositionality result for
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

Next, in Section
7.2
, we use the logical characterisation of Section
3
and
the general
modal decomposition
methodology of Fokkink and van Glabbeek
[
17
]
.
For the operators in the rule format of Section
7.1
, we show how to check the satisfaction
of a logical formula in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}
in a compositional way.

Finally, quiescent behaviour is an important issue in the theory of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
/
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
. In
Section
7.3
we develop a rule format to ensure coherent quiescent behaviour in the
sense of Definition
1
.

The restriction in this section to GSOS rules is partly justified by our wish to have a purely syntactic rule format and by the undecidability results presented in
[
28
]
. In what follows, we assume that the reader is familiar with the standard notions of signature and terms over a signature.

7.1
Congruence rule format

f
of arity
n
in
some signature
\Sigma
is in the

if, and only if, it

A GSOS language is a triple

Note that GSOS rules define operations over states in an arbitrary LTS
with inputs and outputs. In what follows, we apply derived operations
built over the signature of a GSOS language to states in the
collection of LTSs with input and output actions.

Definition 16
.

An operation
f
in a GSOS language is in
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-format if the collection of
f
-defining rules satisfies the
following conditions:

1.

Each
a?
-emitting rule, where
a?
is an input
action, has only output actions as labels of negative premises and
input actions as labels of positive premises.

a?
and each pair of rules

3.

Each

-emitting rule, where

A GSOS language is in
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-format if so is each of
its operations.

Next, we state the main result of this section.

Theorem 21
.

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
is a precongruence for each GSOS language in
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
format.

Proof.

The proof of this result may be found in
A
.
∎

As an example of application of the above result, we show that the
merge operator from
[
6
]
can be expressed in our
rule format.

Example 4
.

Merge, or conjunction, is a composition operator from the theory of

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
. It acts as a logical conjunction of requirements, that is, it
describes systems by a conjunction of sub-systems, or
sub-specifications. We denote by
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}
the result
of the merge of the states
s_{i}
, with
1\leq i\leq n
. In
[
6
]
it is noted that, in general, the merge of two
systems can lead to invalid states (for example the merge of a
quiescent state with another with some output). The solution is to add
a pruning algorithm after calculating the merge. Here we just show the
merge operator and not that pruning algorithm.

The merge operator can be formalised using the following GSOS rules
(one such rule for each
a\in L
):

\frac{\{x_{i}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq n\}}{\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}}\quad\textsc{right={\normalsize.}}

It is immediate to check that the above rules are in

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-format. Therefore the above theorem yields that the merge
operator preserves
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

In the following examples, we discuss whether the rules specifying
(variations on) some classic process-algebraic operations and those
considered in
[
43
]
meet the constraints of our rule format.

Example 5

(Nondeterministic choice)
.

. (Note that the third rule above

, Figure 1]

-format. To see this, consider the

a?
-emitting rules for some input action
a?
, namely

Those rules do not satisfy condition
2
in
Definition
16
. Indeed, the only possible choice for
rule
r^{\prime\prime}
is
r^{\prime}
, which satisfies neither requirement
2a

nor requirement
2c
.

It turns out that nondeterministic choice does not preserve
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}

and the reader will find it easy to construct an example witnessing
the fact that
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
is not a precongruence with respect to
+
by
considering processes that do not satisfy the proviso
of
[
23
, Proposition 6.2]
.

Example 6

(Interleaving)
.

The binary version of the merge operator from Example
4

is essentially the synchronous parallel composition of its
arguments. Another standard notion of parallel composition operator is
one that interleaves the computational steps of its
arguments
[
26
]
. Ignoring quiescence, the following rules
describe the behaviour of the interleaving operator:

\frac{x_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}x_{1}~|~y_{2}}

where
a\in L\setminus\{\delta!\}
.

These rules are not in
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-format. To see this, consider the

a?
-emitting rules for some input action
a?
, namely

r=\frac{x_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y_{1}}{x_{1}~|~x_{2}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y_{1}~|~x_{2}}\quad\text{ and }\quad r^{\prime}=\frac{x_{2}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y_{2}}{x_{1}~|~x_{2}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}x_{1}~|~y_{2}}.

Those rules do not satisfy condition
2
in
Definition
16
. Indeed, the only possible choice for
rule
r^{\prime\prime}
is
r^{\prime}
, which satisfies neither requirement
2a

nor requirement
2c
.

Again, it turns out that the interleaving operation does not preserve

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
and the reader will find it easy to construct an example
witnessing the fact that
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
is not a precongruence with respect
to
|
. The same applies to the unrestricted version of the parallel
composition operator considered in
[
43
, Definition 2.3]
in
the setting of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
. Note that the notion of parallel composition
considered in that reference is a partial operation, as it is only
defined for arguments whose sets of input actions and whose sets of
output actions are disjoint.

Example 7

(Relabelling)
.

In this example, we consider a variation on the CCS relabelling
operator
[
35
]
that is appropriate in the setting of
labelled transition systems with inputs and outputs. A

relabelling
is a function
f:L\rightarrow L
that maps input
actions to input actions, output actions to output actions and such
that
f(\delta!)=\delta!
. The relabelling operator
\_[f]
associated with

is specified using the following rules:

\frac{x\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y}{x[f]\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to19.99997pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle f(a)$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to19.99997pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle f(a)$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to16.66664pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f(a)$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to16.66664pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f(a)$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y[f]}\qquad\text{where $a\in L$.}

If
f
is injective over the set of input actions
I
,
then the above rules are in
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
format and
Theorem
21
yields that the operator
\_[f]
preserves

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

On the other hand, assume that
f(a?)=f(b?)=a?
for two distinct
input actions
a?
and
b?
. Then, the collection of rules for
\_[f]

includes the rules

r=\frac{x\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y}{x[f]\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y[f]}.

Those rules do not satisfy condition
2
in
Definition
16
. Indeed, choosing
r^{\prime}
as
r^{\prime\prime}

violates requirement
2a
and choosing
r
as
r^{\prime\prime}
violates
requirement
2c
. Therefore the rules for
\_[f]
are not in

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-format. The reader will have no trouble in constructing an example
witnessing the fact that, for such a relabelling
f
, the operator

\_[f]
does not preserve
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

Example 8

(Restriction)
.

The restriction
operator
\_\setminus A
, where
A
is included in
L\setminus\{\delta!\}
, is specified using the following rules:

\frac{x\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y}{x\setminus A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to6.66666pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}y\setminus A}\qquad\text{where $a\not\in A$.}

It is easy to see that the above rules are in
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-format and
Theorem
21
yields that the restriction operator

\_\setminus A
preserves
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
.

The main lesson one can draw from the examples we have presented is
that, similarly to
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco}}
, the
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
relation is not algebraically
well behaved. Indeed, a variety of operators only preserve
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
if
one makes some assumptions on the sets of input and output actions of
their arguments.

The operational specification of the hiding operator

\mathbf{hide}~V~\mathbf{in}~\_
, where
V\subseteq O
, presented
in
[
43
, Definition 2.3]
requires an extension of the theory
of
\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
with the internal action
\tau\not\in L
. We briefly
discuss how this extension can be carried out and how to extend the

\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}
-format to cover the hiding operator in
Section
8
.

7.1.1
The rule format cannot be relaxed easily

Here we present some examples showing that the restrictions of the rule format from Definition
16
cannot be relaxed easily.

Example 9
.

This example indicates that the use of input actions in negative
premises of input-emitting rules would invalidate
Theorem
21
. Let
f
be defined by the following
rules:

\frac{}{f(x)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox to9.99998pt{$\leaders{\hbox{\kern-3.06668pt\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}}}{\hfill}$}\kern-5.11108pt\hbox{$\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}}\limits^{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathord{-}$}\kern-3.06668pt\hbox a?$}\kern-3.0pt}}}}}}}f(x)}\quad\textsc{right={\normalsize,}}

where
a?\in I
. Now, let us consider the following processes from Example
1
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
a?
\delta!
\delta!

As remarked in Example
1
,
i~{\mathbin{\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}~s
. On the other
hand,
f(i)~{\mathbin{\kern 10.00002pt\hbox to0.0pt{/\hss}\kern-10.00002pt\mathsf{ioco\underline{s}}}}~f(s)
because
a?\in\mathsf{ins}(f(s))
but

a?\notin\mathsf{ins}(f(i))
.

and the only way that

However, with this choice, the positive premise

of

is not a positive premise of

and

by rule

. The only two possible matching transitions from

(using rule
